setup another review agent that runs the below prompt on all work before it is committed:


<pasted_content id="2765">
Find the defects this Change introduces that its author would want to fix before merging: bugs, regressions, broken invariants, data loss, and missing guards on paths the Change adds or alters.

## How to review

1. Read the repository's review rules: REVIEW.md, AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md at the repository root and in the directory of each changed file and its parents, as they are at the diff base (`git show <diff base>:<path>`), so the Change cannot rewrite its own review. The nearer file wins on conflict. A finding that rests on a rule quotes the rule and cites its file and line.
2. Read the whole diff, then trace each changed path as a running system: its callers, the changed code, and what it calls. Look in particular for:
   - Sequencing and partial failure: state left inconsistent, orphaned or lost when a later step fails or returns early; writes made before a fallible call; cleanup that runs only on success.
   - Assumptions about external or asynchronous state: how another system behaves, or a value that can change between the check and its use.
   - Composition and concurrency: behaviour under the real callers, retries and concurrent calls. Report a race only with an interleaving those callers can produce.
   - Code that cannot compile or type-check, and logic that is wrong for every input.
3. Keep going after the first defect until you have covered the whole diff.
4. Before you report a candidate, try to disprove it by reading the code: open the code it depends on, find the caller or input that triggers it, and check whether a guard elsewhere already prevents it. Drop it if you cannot show the path from the code.

Inspect with read-only commands (git, rg, reading files). Do not install dependencies or run builds or tests.

## What to report

Report a defect only when all of these hold:
- This Change introduced it. Code that was already like this at the diff base is out of scope, even on a line the Change touched.
- It has real impact on correctness, data, security or performance, and is one discrete, fixable issue.
- You can name the input, state or sequence that triggers it. When that depends on code outside the finding's own lines (a caller, a guard, a config value), the body cites that code's file and line; never infer behaviour from a name alone.
- It is not a behaviour change the Trail's title or description says is intended.
- Fixing it asks no more rigor than the surrounding code holds itself to, and the author would fix it if they knew.

Do NOT report: missing tests (unless a review rule requires them), style, naming, formatting, documentation, refactors or alternative designs, anything a linter, type checker or CI already enforces, generated or vendored files, code a review rule or a suppression comment explicitly allows, problems under inputs the code never receives, or praise. Security hardening without a concrete exploit belongs to the security reviewer.

## How to write findings

- One finding per distinct defect. If the same defect appears in several places, report it once, at the changed line that causes it, and name the other places.
- Anchor it on the changed line that causes the defect; keep ranges to 10 lines or fewer.
- Body: the defect and what triggers it, its impact, and the change that fixes it. One short paragraph, at most 3 lines of code, matter-of-fact.
- Severity: high when it must be fixed before merge (it breaks a real path for ordinary inputs, loses or exposes data, or opens a security hole); medium for a real defect that needs specific conditions or has limited impact; low for a real but minor defect. Never overstate it.
- Confidence: 0.9 or more when you traced the triggering path in the code; 0.7 to 0.9 with strong evidence and one assumption left; 0.5 to 0.7 when one condition could not be confirmed. Do not report anything below 0.5.
- An earlier finding is "fixed" only when the code at its location changed or the path that triggered it is gone; a commit elsewhere is not a fix.

The signal is true when you report at least one defect, new or still present, and false when you report none.
</pasted_content id="2765">

<summary>Agent "TUI dogfood audit" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I ran the TUI for real and wrote up 27 defects in `docs/v1.1.4/TUI-AUDIT.md`, which is now **PR #444** ("docs(v1.1.4): TUI dogfood audit", branch `v1/v114-tui-audit`). The audit changed no code. The PR also includes the fake OpenAI server and tmux helper scripts, in `docs/v1.1.4/tui-audit-harness/`.

I used the real CLI with the real sidecar in tmux against the local fake server. Sizes were 80x24, 100x30, 120x40, 200x60, with live resize. Not tested: a real GUI terminal, mouse wheel and OSC 52 copy, because tmux can't produce them. The fake model also answers faster than a real one.

By severity: 1 blocker, 9 major, 10 minor, 7 polish.

**Top 10 defects**
1. **D-01, blocker: approval prompt ignores y, n and Enter.** Several overlapping timing guards drop keys with no feedback. Keyboard approval only worked as Left, wait 2.3 s, Enter, wait 2.3 s, Enter. The three drawn buttons don't match the keys that reach them. With a real model the first panel is less likely to hit this, but fast local models and back-to-back approvals still do (`live_state.rs:362-373, 1237, 1342-1440`).
2. **D-02, major: the view drifts while you scroll back during a stream.** I sampled it moving from line 81 to 174. The anchor stores the distance to the end of the streaming entry, which grows (`live_view.rs:1246-1256`).
3. **D-03, major: every fresh repo shows "●1 changed".** The TUI's own `.nexus/` directory is counted as a change, and `/git` lists it. `/diff` says the tree is clean, so the header and `/diff` disagree.
4. **D-04, major: the turn verdict contradicts itself.** "✓ approved verifier npm test" is followed by "no verifier ran". A denied delete still ends with "Files changed" (`live_state.rs:2296`).
5. **D-05, major: no live token or context indicator.** The session id also vanishes from the header after the first turn. goose shows context usage; opencode has a usage footer.
6. **D-06, major: provider errors spam the transcript.** A 429 retries forever with one identical line every 2 s. The 500 error shows internal terms such as "replay budget" and raw JSON.
7. **D-07, major: first run with no provider is a dead end.** It prints a raw ECONNREFUSED chain, and a prompt then spins in a retry loop without telling the user to connect a provider.
8. **D-08, major: a stalled stream is never detected.** It showed "thinking" for 1:35 with no warning or timeout.
9. **D-09, major: Shift+Enter is advertised everywhere but works only on kitty-protocol terminals.** Elsewhere it submits a half-written prompt, and xterm's modifyOtherKeys form is not recognised (`live.rs:335-345`).
10. **D-10, major: no mouse support.** In the alternate screen the wheel can turn into Up/Down and recall history over the draft. I could not verify this in tmux, so it is an inference. opencode defaults mouse capture on.

**Held up well:** idle CPU is 0.0-0.7%, rendering is differential with no flicker, and unicode editing is grapheme-correct. `NO_COLOR` is honoured, terminal restore on exit is clean, and sidecar-crash recovery works.

One observation I couldn't reproduce, a first SIGTERM that seemed ignored, is recorded in the report but not counted. D-20 (a spurious "provider unavailable" on a healthy server) is counted as a minor but flagged unconfirmed, since it may be a fake-server artefact.

For peer comparison, `goose-docs` isn't an index on the XERJ node (`goose` is). The `opencode` index was flagged "not verified complete", so a miss there would not prove anything.</result>

  const text = String(JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(`${work}/${agent}.json`, "utf8")).result ?? "").trimEnd();

  const open = text.lastIndexOf("```json");
  const close = text.lastIndexOf("```");

  try {

  }
}

  fs.copyFileSync(`${work}/${agent}.json`, kept);

<result>WP-1308 is open as PR #443 (not merged), head `38a2a119` on `v1/v114-wp1308-skills`, with the Reference coding section in the body.

**What it does**
- **Skill note:** each operator message is scored against trusted skills' names and descriptions with a local lexical ranker (BM25, no model call, no new dependency). Up to 5 matches go in one `[Nexus skill note]` user message directly after that operator message, capped at 2% of the window. The system prompt and earlier messages are never touched, so every request extends the previous one.
- **Lookup:** `activate_skill` with a name no skill has now searches and returns ranked name and description. It activates nothing, and untrusted skills are never scored. I used `activate_skill` rather than a new `find_skills` tool, so no extra tool schema.
- **Carried #409 items:**
  - A pre-#409 saved session is recognised by its catalog heading and cut to the window on resume.
  - The new catalog is recorded only after a real replacement.
  - `getPromptSection` builds its final listing once.
  - First discovery and resume are tested directly.
  - The not-found list of every skill name is replaced by the search, and the catalog hint now points at it.
  - A catalog that shrank to nothing stays absent. I made that call on purpose, because notes and lookup cover those windows and rewriting `messages[0]` drops the prefix cache. It is recorded in FINDINGS.
- **One extra fix:** a note after a very large operator message turned that message into an "earlier request" that compaction could condense instead of refusing the turn. A message taking a quarter of the window therefore gets no note. Refused turns withdraw the message and its note together.

**Commits:** four, one concern each (ranker and formatter, carried catalog fixes, per-prompt note, lookup), then a hint-text test update and the FINDINGS section E rows. Commit trailer and PR footer are as you specified.

**Red then green**
- **`skill_ranking.test.ts` (6 tests):** red because the module import failed with the library stashed; green after.
- **`skill_injection.test.ts` and `skill_lookup.test.ts`:** red before the agent change. The note tests failed (no note was added). The lookup tests failed with `Error ... Available skills: kafka-0, ...`. All 9 pass after.
- **`skill_catalog_resume.test.ts`:** the pre-#409 resume and "miss not recorded" tests failed before the fix and pass after. The "first discovery" test was red only because of a fixture mistake of mine, so it adds direct coverage but no real red evidence.
- **Also green:** `skills_agents_dir`, `skill_catalog_budget`, `agent_runtime_identity`, `capabilities`, `workspace_trust`, `approval_policy`, and `harness.test.ts` (34 tests, 33 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped).

**Measurement:** I used a synthetic 305-skill host with a 64K window. The full catalog is 157,895 chars and the bounded catalog is 3,905. The note for a postgres prompt is 2,359 chars (budget 3,932) and names 5 postgres skills.

**Not verified**
- Your original 305 skills were not available, so whether the matches for the original citable prompt are plausible is unchecked.
- I did not run the full `make test-ci` gate or the other heavy suites.
- I did not check CI on the PR.
- XERJ was reachable. The opencode corpus reported itself as not verified complete, and `goose-docs` is not a prefix on this node (`goose` is).
- The XERJ field report (`xerj feedback`) was not sent.
